With s = "aaabbbb", adding 2 chars, we can get something like "aaabbbbc" or "aaabbbba" or "aaabbbab", etc.

To have a tandem repeat of length 8, we need 4 characters equal to the next 4 characters.

If we start at position 0: we need positions 0-3 to equal positions 4-7.
Original: "aaab" (positions 0-3), then need positions 4-7 to be "aaab"
But original has "aaabbbb", so position 4 is 'b', not 'a'. So we can't make positions 4-7 "aaab" with just adding 2 characters.

If we start at position 1: we need positions 1-4 to equal positions 5-8.
Position 1: 'a', 2: 'a', 3: 'b', 4: 'b' → first half is "aabb"
We need positions 5-8 to be "aabb". 
Position 5 would be the 5th character of original string, original has s[4]='b', s[5]='b', s[6]='b', s[7]='b'
With k=2 added, we have 9 characters total, so positions 5-8 exist.
We can set positions 5,6,7,8 to be "aabb"? But we can only change the last 2 characters.

We can only add 2 characters at the end, so we can only control positions len(s) to len(s)+k-1 = 7 to 8 (0-indexed).

So we can only set positions 7 and 8.

For the tandem repeat starting at position 1: we need positions 1-4 to equal positions 5-8.
- positions 1-4: s[1]='a', s[2]='a', s[3]='b', s[4]='b' → "aabb"
- positions 5-8: s[5]='b', s[6]='b', s[7]='b', s[8]=? 
We need positions 5,6,7,8 to be "aabb"
But s[5]='b' ≠ 'a', so we can't change it.

The key constraint is that the part of the tandem repeat that overlaps with the original string cannot be changed arbitrarily - only the added k characters can be changed.

So for a tandem repeat of length 2n starting at position i, we need:
- For j from 0 to n-1: character at position (i+j) should equal character at position (i+j+n)
- For positions that are in the added part (positions >= len(s)), we can choose any character
- For positions < len(s), we must match the original string

Therefore, for each possible n and each possible starting position i, we check if the tandem repeat is possible.

The maximum n we can achieve is limited by 2n ≤ len(s) + k, so n ≤ (len(s) + k) // 2.

But we also need to ensure there's a valid starting position i such that for all j in [0, n-1]:
- If i+j < len(s): character at i+j must equal character at i+j+n
- If i+j >= len(s): character at i+j can be set to match, but we need to check if i+j+n is within bounds
- If i+j+n >= len(s): character at i+j+n must be consistent with the original string or can be chosen

Actually, let's refine this:

For a tandem repeat of length 2n starting at position i:
- The substring is from index i to i+2n-1
- For each position j in 0 to n-1:
  - Position i+j and position i+j+n must be equal
- Position i+j is at index (i+j)
- Position i+j+n is at index (i+j+n)

So we need:
- If i+j < len(s) and i+j+n >= len(s): then character at i+j must equal character at i+j+n, but i+j+n may not be original, so we can only constrain if i+j+n is in original string
- Actually, if i+j+n < len(s), then both positions are in original string, so s[i+j] must equal s[i+j+n]
- If i+j < len(s) and i+j+n >= len(s): then s[i+j] must equal the character at position (i+j+n) which is in the added part, and we can choose that character
- If i+j >= len(s) and i+j+n >= len(s): both are in added part, so we can set both characters to match (as long as the length allows)

So the only constraints are:
1. If i+j < len(s) and i+j+n < len(s): s[i+j] must equal s[i+j+n]
2. If i+j < len(s) and i+j+n >= len(s): we can choose the added character, so no constraint from original string
3. If i+j >= len(s) and i+j+n >= len(s): both are in added part, we can set them to match

Therefore, for a tandem repeat to exist at position i with parameter n, we need to check for each j in 0 to n-1:
- If i+j < len(s) and i+j+n < len(s): s[i+j] == s[i+j+n]
- If i+j < len(s) and i+j+n >= len(s): can be satisfied by choosing appropriate added character
- If i+j >= len(s) and i+j+n >= len(s): can be satisfied by choosing appropriate added character

The only constraints that force values are when both i+j and i+j+n are in the original string (i+j < len(s) and i+j+n < len(s)).

For this to be possible, we need that for all j in 0 to n-1 where i+j < len(s) and i+j+n < len(s), we have s[i+j] == s[i+j+n].

Also, we need i+2n-1 < len(s) + k, which means i+2n-1 <= len(s) + k - 1, so i+2n-1 <= len(s) + k - 1.

Since we want to maximize n, we can try n from 1 to (len(s) + k) // 2, and for each n, check if there exists a starting position i such that:
- i >= 0
- i+2n-1 < len(s) + k (so the entire tandem repeat fits)
- For all j in [0, n-1]: if i+j < len(s) and i+j+n < len(s), then s[i+j] == s[i+j+n]

Then the answer is the maximum such 2n.

Let me verify with example 2:

So for n=3, we have a valid tandem repeat starting at i=3, length 6.

Example 3: s="abracadabra", k=10
len(s)=11, k=10, total length=21

Can we have a tandem repeat of length 20?

- If i+j < 11 and i+j+10 < 11: need s[i+j] == s[i+j+10]

For j≥1: i+j≥1, so i+j+10≥11 → so i+j+10 ≥ 11 → it's in added part, no constraint
So only constraint is at j=0: s[0]==s[10] → 'a'=='a' ✓

So i=0, n=10 is valid → length 20 ✓

Therefore, the algorithm is:

1. Read s and k
2. len_s = len(s)
3. max_n = (len_s + k) // 2
4. For n from max_n down to 1:  # start from maximum possible n and go down to find the largest valid
   - For each starting position i from 0 to (len_s + k - 1 - 2*n) inclusive:
     - Check if for all j in 0 to n-1:
       - If i+j < len_s and i+j+n < len_s: s[i+j] must equal s[i+j+n]
       - Otherwise, no constraint (we can adjust the added characters)
     - If such i exists, return 2*n
5. If no n found, return 0 (though n≥1 by constraints)

Actually, we can optimize: start from max_n down to 1, and for each n, check all valid i.

Since len_s and k are at most 200, and n ≤ 100, and i ≤ len_s + k - 2*n, the total iteration is reasonable.

Let me implement this.

Wait, in the tandem repeat of length 2n, the total length is 2n, so the last position is i+2n-1, which must be < len_s + k.

So i ≤ (len_s + k - 1) - (2n - 1) = len_s + k - 2n

So valid i range: 0 to len_s + k - 2n

We can write:

for n from max_n down to 1:
    for i from 0 to len_s + k - 2*n:
        valid = True
        for j in range(n):
            pos1 = i + j
            pos2 = i + j + n
            if pos1 < len_s and pos2 < len_s:
                if s[pos1] != s[pos2]:
                    valid = False
                    break
        if valid:
            return 2*n

But we need to make sure that pos2 < len_s + k, which it will be because pos2 = i+j+n ≤ (len_s + k - 2n) + (n-1) + n = len_s + k - 2n + 2n - 1 = len_s + k - 1, so pos2 ≤ len_s + k - 1.

Actually, pos2 = i + j + n ≤ (len_s + k - 2n) + (n-1) + n = len_s + k - 2n + 2n - 1 = len_s + k - 1, which is correct.

And for the constraint, when pos1 < len_s and pos2 >= len_s, we can set pos2 to match pos1 (since we can choose added characters).

When pos1 >= len_s and pos2 >= len_s, both are in added part, so we can set them to match.

So only when both are in original string do we have a constraint.

Therefore, the code is:

```python
s = input().strip()
k = int(input().strip())
len_s = len(s)

max_n = (len_s + k) // 2

# Try from maximum possible n down to 1
for n in range(max_n, 0, -1):
    # The tandem repeat starts at i, ends at i + 2*n - 1
    # So i + 2*n - 1 < len_s + k
    # i < len_s + k - 2*n + 1
    # So i ranges from 0 to len_s + k - 2*n
    max_i = len_s + k - 2 * n
    if max_i < 0:
        continue
        
    found = False
    for i in range(max_i + 1):  # i from 0 to max_i inclusive
        valid = True
        for j in range(n):
            pos1 = i + j
            pos2 = i + j + n
            # If both positions are within the original string, they must match
            if pos1 < len_s and pos2 < len_s:
                if s[pos1] != s[pos2]:
                    valid = False
                    break
        if valid:
            found = True
            break
            
    if found:
        print(2 * n)
        exit()

# Should not reach here given constraints
print(0)
```

Problem

View Problem Statement 
You are an expert Python programmer. You will be given a question (problem specification) and will generate a correct Python program that matches the specification and passes all tests.

Kolya got string s for his birthday, the string consists of small English letters. He immediately added k more characters to the right of the string.

Then Borya came and said that the new string contained a tandem repeat of length l as a substring. How large could l be?

See notes for definition of a tandem repeat.


-----Input-----

The first line contains s (1 ≤ |s| ≤ 200). This string contains only small English letters. The second line contains number k (1 ≤ k ≤ 200) — the number of the added characters.


-----Output-----

Print a single number — the maximum length of the tandem repeat that could have occurred in the new string.


-----Examples-----
Input
aaba
2

Output
6

Input
aaabbbb
2

Output
6

Input
abracadabra
10

Output
20



-----Note-----

A tandem repeat of length 2n is string s, where for any position i (1 ≤ i ≤ n) the following condition fulfills: s_{i} = s_{i} + n.

In the first sample Kolya could obtain a string aabaab, in the second — aaabbbbbb, in the third — abracadabrabracadabra.### Format: Read the inputs from stdin solve the problem and write the answer to stdout (do not directly test on the sample inputs). I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have a string `s` and add `k` more characters to the right
2. We need to find the maximum length of a tandem repeat possible in the resulting string
3. A tandem repeat of length `2n` means a string where the first `n` characters are identical to the next `n` characters

## Key Insights

1. The tandem repeat can start at any position in the final string
2. The tandem repeat length must be even (2n)
3. We can choose what characters to add (k characters), so we have flexibility
4. We want to maximize the tandem repeat length

## Strategy

1. The final string is `s + (k characters we can choose)`
2. For a tandem repeat of length `2n` to exist, we need to find a position where two identical halves of length `n` appear consecutively
3. We can try all possible tandem repeat lengths from maximum possible down to 2
4. For each possible tandem repeat length `2n`, check if it's possible to achieve by placing the tandem repeat somewhere in the final string

- We need to see if we can place such a repeat using the original string and the k added characters

Since we can choose the k added characters freely, we have control over the last k positions.

So the constraint is: for each p in [i, i+n-1], if p+n < len(s), then we must have s[p+n] = s[p]

Otherwise, if p+n >= len(s), we can set the character at position p+n to match s[p], which we can do.

Therefore, for a given length 2n and starting position i, it's possible if and only if for all p in [i, i+n-1], if p+n < len(s), then s[p+n] = s[p].

Additionally, we need i+2n-1 < len(s) + k, so that the tandem repeat fits in the string.

So algorithm:
1. For each possible length 2n (from max possible down to 2), where max possible = 2 * min(len(s)+k, something)
   - Actually, max possible 2n is 2 * min(n where we have enough space)
   - The maximum 2n is 2 * min(len(s)+k - i, n) for some i, so maximum 2n is 2 * (len(s) + k) but we also need to check constraints
   - Actually, 2n must be <= len(s) + k, so n <= (len(s) + k) // 2
   - So n from 1 to (len(s) + k) // 2
2. For each n from 1 to (len(s) + k) // 2:
   - For each starting position i from 0 to len(s) + k - 2n:
     - Check if for every position p in [i, i+n-1], if p+n < len(s), then s[p+n] == s[p]
     - If yes, then we found a tandem repeat of length 2n
3. Return the maximum such 2n found

But we want the maximum possible length, so we can iterate n from max_possible down to 1 and return the first valid one.

Example 3: s="abracadabra", k=10, so total length 21

## Implementation

```python
s = input().strip()
k = int(input().strip())
n_len = len(s)
total_len = n_len + k

# We want maximum tandem repeat length 2n
max_length = 0

# Try all possible n from large to small
# n can be at most total_len // 2
max_n = total_len // 2
for n in range(max_n, 0, -1):
    length_2n = 2 * n
    # Check all possible starting positions i
    for i in range(0, total_len - length_2n + 1):
        valid = True
        # Check each position p in [i, i+n-1]
        for p in range(i, i + n):
            # Position p+n in the original string
            pos2 = p + n
            # If pos2 is within the original string, we need s[pos2] == s[p]
            if pos2 < n_len:
                if s[pos2] != s[p]:
                    valid = False
                    break
        if valid:
            max_length = length_2n
            break
    if max_length > 0:
        break

print(max_length)
```

Wait, I see a potential issue: in the inner loop, when we check s[pos2], pos2 = p+n, but p starts from i, so pos2 starts from i+n, which might be >= n_len and thus outside.

But in our constraint, we only check if pos2 < n_len, which is correct.

Also, when pos2 >= n_len, it means that position p+n is in the added part, and we can set it to match s[p], so no constraint.

So the code looks correct.

Problem

View Problem Statement 
You are an expert Python programmer. You will be given a question (problem specification) and will generate a correct Python program that matches the specification and passes all tests.

Kolya got string s for his birthday, the string consists of small English letters. He immediately added k more characters to the right of the string.

Then Borya came and said that the new string contained a tandem repeat of length l as a substring. How large could l be?

See notes for definition of a tandem repeat.


-----Input-----

The first line contains s (1 ≤ |s| ≤ 200). This string contains only small English letters. The second line contains number k (1 ≤ k ≤ 200) — the number of the added characters.


-----Output-----

Print a single number — the maximum length of the tandem repeat that could have occurred in the new string.


-----Examples-----
Input
aaba
2

Output
6

Input
aaabbbb
2

Output
6

Input
abracadabra
10

Output
20



-----Note-----

A tandem repeat of length 2n is string s, where for any position i (1 ≤ i ≤ n) the following condition fulfills: s_{i} = s_{i} + n.

In the first sample Kolya could obtain a string aabaab, in the second — aaabbbbbb, in the third — abracadabrabracadabra.### Format: Read the inputs from stdin solve the problem and write the answer to stdout (do not directly test on the sample inputs). I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have a string `s` and we add `k` more characters to the right
2. We need to find the maximum length of a tandem repeat that could appear in the resulting string
3. A tandem repeat of length `2n` means a substring where the first half equals the second half

## Key Insights

1. A tandem repeat of length `2n` means we have a substring `t` of length `2n` where `t[0:n] == t[n:2n]`
2. We can choose which `k` characters to add (they can be any letters)
3. To maximize the tandem repeat length, we want to find the longest possible `2n` such that we can make a substring of length `2n` where the first half equals the second half
4. Since we can choose the k added characters freely, we can always make the second half of any tandem repeat match the first half by choosing appropriate characters

## Strategy

1. For each possible tandem repeat length `2n` (from large to small), check if it's possible to have such a repeat in the resulting string
2. The tandem repeat can start at any position from 0 to `len(s) + k - 2n`
3. For a tandem repeat of length `2n` starting at position `i`, the first half is from `i` to `i+n-1` and the second half is from `i+n` to `i+2n-1`
4. We need to ensure that:
   - The first half of the tandem repeat (positions `i` to `i+n-1`) can be formed using existing characters (if they exist) or by adding characters (if they need to be added)
   - The second half of the tandem repeat (positions `i+n` to `i+2n-1`) must be covered by the available positions
   - If any position in the second half is beyond the original string length, we need to ensure we can add characters to make them match the first half

5. Since we can choose the added characters freely, we can always make a tandem repeat of length `2n` if:
   - The tandem repeat starts at a valid position (the second half doesn't go beyond the total length)
   - The first half is within the available positions (original string or added characters)

6. For a tandem repeat of length `2n` to be possible:
   - The end of the tandem repeat is at position `i + 2n - 1`
   - This position must be ≤ `len(s) + k - 1`
   - For positions in the first half (`i` to `i+n-1`), if they are within original string, they must match what's there; if they're in the added part, we can set them to whatever we want
   - For positions in the second half (`i+n` to `i+2n-1`), we need to ensure we have enough added characters to cover them

## Algorithm

1. Try all possible tandem repeat lengths from large to small (starting from `2 * (len(s) + k) // 2` down to 2)
2. For each length `2n`, try all possible starting positions `i`
3. For each starting position, check if the tandem repeat can be formed:
   - The second half ends at `i + 2n - 1`, which must be ≤ `len(s) + k - 1`
   - For positions in the second half that are beyond the original string length, we need to check if we have enough remaining added characters
4. We can always form a tandem repeat if the second half can be formed with the available characters (since we can choose them freely)

Wait, let me reconsider. Since we can choose the k added characters freely, if there's any position in the tandem repeat that falls in the added part, we can make it match the first half of the tandem repeat.

So a tandem repeat of length `2n` is possible if there exists a starting position `i` such that:
- `i + 2n - 1 ≤ len(s) + k - 1` (the tandem repeat fits in the total string)
- For each position `j` in `[i, i+n-1]` (first half):
  - If `j < len(s)`, the character at position `j` must be compatible with what we want (it's fixed)
  - If `j >= len(s)`, we can choose the character freely
- For each position `j` in `[i+n, i+2n-1]` (second half):
  - If `j >= len(s)`, we can choose the character freely
  - If `j < len(s)`, we need to make sure the character at position `j` in the original string matches the corresponding character in the first half

Actually, since we can choose the added characters freely, we only need to ensure that for all positions `j` in `[i+n, i+2n-1]` that are within the original string, the character at position `j` in the original string must equal the character at position `j - n` in the first half.

But wait - the tandem repeat requires that `t[i+j] = t[i+j+n]` for all `j` in `[0, n-1]`.

So for each position `j` in `[0, n-1]`, we need `t[i+j] = t[i+j+n]`.

If `i+j < len(s)`, then `t[i+j]` is the original character at position `i+j`.
If `i+j >= len(s)`, then `t[i+j]` can be chosen freely.

Similarly, if `i+j+n < len(s)`, then `t[i+j+n]` is the original character at position `i+j+n`.
If `i+j+n >= len(s)`, then `t[i+j+n]` can be chosen freely.

So for each pair `(i+j, i+j+n)` where `j < n`:
- If `i+j < len(s)` and `i+j+n < len(s)`, then we need `s[i+j] == s[i+j+n]`
- If `i+j < len(s)` and `i+j+n >= len(s)`, then we can choose the added character to match `s[i+j]`
- If `i+j >= len(s)` and `i+j+n < len(s)`, this is impossible since `i+j >= len(s)` but `i+j+n < len(s)` implies `n < 0`
- If `i+j >= len(s)` and `i+j+n >= len(s)`, then we can choose both characters to match each other (by setting `t[i+j+n] = t[i+j]`)

The only constraint is when both positions are within the original string, and their characters don't match.

So the algorithm is:
1. For each possible tandem repeat length `2n` (from large to small):
   - For each possible starting position `i` from 0 to `len(s) + k - 2n`:
     - Check if the tandem repeat can be formed
     - For each `j` in `[0, n-1]`:
       - Let pos1 = i + j, pos2 = i + j + n
       - If pos1 < len(s) and pos2 < len(s) and s[pos1] != s[pos2]:
         - This position pair is invalid
       - Otherwise, it's valid (we can either match the existing chars or choose the added ones)
     - If all pairs are valid, then `2n` is achievable
2. Return the maximum `2n` found

```python
s = input().strip()
k = int(input().strip())

n = len(s)
total_len = n + k

# We'll try tandem repeat lengths from large to small
# Maximum possible length is 2 * min(n+k, something)
max_len = 0

# Try all possible n values (half-length of tandem repeat)
# Maximum n is (total_len // 2)
max_n = total_len // 2

# Try from large n to small
for n_val in range(max_n, 0, -1):
    len_tandem = 2 * n_val
    # Try all starting positions i
    for i in range(0, total_len - len_tandem + 1):
        valid = True
        # Check each position pair in the tandem repeat
        for j in range(n_val):
            pos1 = i + j
            pos2 = i + j + n_val
            
            # If both positions are within original string
            if pos1 < n and pos2 < n:
                if s[pos1] != s[pos2]:
                    valid = False
                    break
            # If pos1 is in original string but pos2 is beyond
            elif pos1 < n and pos2 >= n:
                # pos1 has fixed value, pos2 is in added part → we can choose it
                # So we need to match s[pos1] to what we set at pos2
                # This is always possible since we can choose added characters
                continue
            # If pos1 is beyond original string (in added part) and pos2 is in original
            elif pos1 >= n and pos2 < n:
                # pos1 is in added part (we can choose), pos2 has fixed value
                # But pos1 >= n and pos2 < n means pos1 >= n > pos2, so pos1 > pos2
                # But we need s[pos1] = s[pos2], where pos1 >= n, pos2 < n
                # This means we want to set a character at pos1 to equal s[pos2]
                # But since we can choose the added characters, we can do this
                continue
            # If both are beyond original string
            elif pos1 >= n and pos2 >= n:
                # Both are in added part → we can choose both, so we can make them equal
                continue
        
        if valid:
            max_len = len_tandem
            break
    
    if max_len > 0:
        break

print(max_len)
```

Wait, I think I have an error in the condition. In the case where pos1 >= n and pos2 < n, this should never happen because if pos1 >= n, then since i + j >= n, and j >= 0, then i + j + n_val > i + j >= n, so pos2 = i + j + n_val > i + j >= n, so pos2 >= n.

Let me verify: if i + j >= n, then i + j + n_val >= n + n_val >= n > pos2? No.

Actually: if i + j >= n, then i + j + n_val >= n + n_val > n (since n_val >= 1), so pos2 >= n + n_val > n.

So pos2 >= n when pos1 >= n.

Therefore, the case where pos1 >= n and pos2 < n is impossible.

Similarly, if pos2 < n, then pos1 = pos2 - n_val < n - n_val ≤ n - 1 < n.

So the only possible cases are:
- Both in original string: need s[pos1] == s[pos2]
- pos1 in original, pos2 in added: always possible (we can choose pos2)
- pos1 in added, pos2 in original: impossible (as shown above)
- both in added: always possible

But the case pos1 in added and pos2 in original: this would require pos1 >= n and pos2 < n.
But pos1 = i + j >= n, pos2 = i + j + n_val >= n + n_val > n (since n_val >= 1), so pos2 >= n + n_val >= n + 1 > n.
So pos2 cannot be < n if pos1 >= n.

Therefore, only two cases:
1. Both in original string: need s[pos1] == s[pos2]
2. Everything else: always possible

So I can simplify the condition:

```python
s = input().strip()
k = int(input().strip())

n_len = len(s)
total_len = n_len + k

max_len = 0

# Try tandem repeat length from largest possible down to 2
max_n = total_len // 2
for n_val in range(max_n, 0, -1):
    tandem_len = 2 * n_val
    for start_pos in range(total_len - tandem_len + 1):
        valid = True
        for j in range(n_val):
            pos1 = start_pos + j
            pos2 = start_pos + j + n_val
            
            # If both positions are in original string, they must match
            if pos1 < n_len and pos2 < n_len:
                if s[pos1] != s[pos2]:
                    valid = False
                    break
        
        if valid:
            max_len = tandem_len
            break
    
    if max_len > 0:
        break

print(max_len)
```

View Problem Statement 
You are an expert Python programmer. You will be given a question (problem specification) and will generate a correct Python program that matches the specification and passes all tests.

Kolya got string s for his birthday, the string consists of small English letters. He immediately added k more characters to the right of the string.

Then Borya came and said that the new string contained a tandem repeat of length l as a substring. How large could l be?

See notes for definition of a tandem repeat.


-----Input-----

The first line contains s (1 ≤ |s| ≤ 200). This string contains only small English letters. The second line contains number k (1 ≤ k ≤ 200) — the number of the added characters.


-----Output-----

Print a single number — the maximum length of the tandem repeat that could have occurred in the new string.


-----Examples-----
Input
aaba
2

Output

Input

2

Output
6

Input

10

Output
20



-----Note-----

A tandem repeat of length 2n is string s, where for any position i (1 ≤ i ≤ n) the following condition fulfills: s_{i} = s_{i} + n.

In the first sample Kolya could obtain a string aabaab, in the second — aaabbbbbb, in the third — abracadabrabracadabra.### Format: Read the inputs from stdin solve the problem and write the answer to stdout (do not directly test on the sample inputs). I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have a string `s` and we add `k` characters to its right
2. After adding these characters, there exists a tandem repeat of length `l` as a substring
3. We need to find the maximum possible value of `l`

A tandem repeat of length `2n` means a string where the first half is identical to the second half. So if we have a tandem repeat of length `l = 2n`, then positions `1` to `n` are identical to positions `n+1` to `2n`.

## Key Insights

1. The tandem repeat can start at any position in the final string (original string + k added characters)
2. The tandem repeat can be of any length `2n` where `n` is the half-length
3. We want to maximize `l = 2n`
4. We can choose what k characters to add, so we have control over the suffix of the final string

## Strategy

1. The tandem repeat must be contained within the final string of length `len(s) + k`
2. For a tandem repeat of length `2n` to exist, we need at least `2n` characters available
3. So `2n ≤ len(s) + k`, which means `n ≤ (len(s) + k) // 2`, so maximum `l = 2 * ((len(s) + k) // 2)`
4. However, we also need to check if such a tandem repeat can actually be formed given the original string and our choice of added characters

So how to find the maximum possible?

Key insight: Since we can choose the k added characters, we can always create a tandem repeat of length `2n` if:
1. `2n ≤ len(s) + k`
2. We can position the tandem repeat such that the first half is "available" and we can set the second half to match

But the first half might contain only part of the original string or only part of the added characters.

Actually, for a tandem repeat of length `2n` starting at position `i`:
- The first half occupies positions `i` to `i+n-1`
- The second half occupies positions `i+n` to `i+2n-1`

For the first half:
- If `i+n-1 < len(s)`, then positions `i` to `i+n-1` come from the original string
- Otherwise, some or all come from added characters

For the second half:
- It starts at position `i+n`
- If `i+n < len(s)`, it overlaps with the original string
- Otherwise, it's entirely in added characters

Since we can choose the added characters, we can always make the second half match the first half, as long as there's no conflict with the original string's fixed characters.

For a tandem repeat of length `2n` to be possible:
- We need `2n ≤ len(s) + k`
- We need to find some starting position `i` such that the first half and second half don't contain conflicting predetermined characters from the original string that would prevent the two halves from being equal

But since we control the added characters, the only restriction is that if a position in the second half is before we add characters, it's fixed and must match the corresponding position in the first half.

Wait, this is getting complicated.

Alternative approach:

Since we can choose the k added characters, for any tandem repeat of length `2n` that starts at position `i` where `i+n-1 < len(s)`, we can only create it if for every position in the first half that is in the original string, the corresponding position in the second half (which might be in the original or added) matches.

But if the second half has positions that are in the original string, those are fixed and must match the first half's characters.

However, because we control the added characters, we can only have a conflict if a position in the second half is in the original string and doesn't match the required value from the first half.

So for a specific tandem repeat placement at position `i` of length `2n`:
- For each position `j` in `0 ≤ j < n`:
  - First half position: `i+j`
  - Second half position: `i+n+j`
  - If `i+j < len(s)` and `i+n+j < len(s)`: both are in original string, so `s[i+j]` must equal `s[i+n+j]`
  - If `i+j < len(s)` and `i+n+j >= len(s)`: first half from original, second half from added. We can set the added character to match → always possible
  - If `i+j >= len(s)` and `i+n+j < len(s)`: both in added, so we can set both to match → always possible
  - If `i+j >= len(s)` and `i+n+j >= len(s)`: both in added, can set to match → always possible

The only constraint is when both positions are in the original string and the characters don't match.

Therefore, for a tandem repeat of length `2n` to be possible, there must exist some position `i` such that for all `j` where both `i+j` and `i+n+j` are in `[0, len(s)-1]`, we have `s[i+j] == s[i+n+j]`.

And `2n ≤ len(s) + k`.

To maximize `2n`, we need to find the maximum `n` such that there exists a starting position `i` in `[0, len(s)+k-2n]` where for all `j` in `[0, n-1]`, if both `i+j < len(s)` and `i+n+j < len(s)`, then `s[i+j] == s[i+n+j]`.

But since we want to maximize `l = 2n`, and `2n ≤ len(s) + k`, the upper bound is `2 * ((len(s) + k) // 2)`.

We need to check if this upper bound is achievable, or if we need to go lower.

We can try all possible values of `n` from `max_possible` down to 1, and for each `n`, check if there exists a starting position `i` such that the tandem repeat of length `2n` starting at `i` can be formed (i.e., for all j where both i+j and i+n+j are in the original string, s[i+j] == s[i+n+j]).

However, since the string is small (up to 200), we can just iterate over all possible tandem repeat lengths from `2*min(len(s)+k, something)` down to 2, and check if any such tandem repeat is possible.

Algorithm:
1. Let total_length = len(s) + k
2. For length from total_length down to 2 (step 2, since tandem repeat length is even):
   - For each possible start position i from 0 to total_length - length:
     - Check if we can form a tandem repeat of this length starting at i
     - This means for each j in 0 to (length//2 - 1):
       - position1 = i + j
       - position2 = i + length//2 + j
       - If position1 < len(s) and position2 < len(s): we need s[position1] == s[position2]
       - Otherwise (one or both beyond original string), we can choose the values (since we can add characters), so no conflict
     - If we found such a valid i, return length
3. If no such tandem repeat found, return 2 (minimum possible)

- j=2: pos1=2, pos2=5 → s[2]='a', s[5]='b' → a!=b → invalid

i=1: positions 1-6
- j=0: pos1=1, pos2=4 → s[1]='a', s[4]='b' → a!=b → invalid
- j=1: pos1=2, pos2=5 → s[2]='a', s[5]='b' → a!=b → invalid
- j=2: pos1=3, pos2=6 → s[3]='b', s[6]='b' → b==b ✓

- So valid! So tandem repeat of length 6 is possible.

But is length 4 possible? Yes, but we want maximum.

Is length 6 achievable? Yes, as shown.

Can we achieve length 8? We just showed no.

Why did my earlier manual placement work? I placed it at i=3, but for i=3 and length=8, the positions go to i+7=10, which is beyond total_length=9.

So the constraint is that start position i must satisfy i + length - 1 < total_length.

I forgot this constraint.

In my algorithm, I need to ensure that for a given start position i and length L, we have i + L - 1 < total_length.

So algorithm:

1. total_length = len(s) + k
2. For L from total_length down to 2, step 2:
   - For start i from 0 to total_length - L:
     - valid = True
     - n = L // 2
     - for j in range(n):
         pos1 = i + j
         pos2 = i + n + j
         if pos1 < len(s) and pos2 < len(s):
             if s[pos1] != s[pos2]:
                 valid = False
                 break
     - if valid:
         return L
3. return 2

s = input().strip()
k = int(input().strip())

total_length = len(s) + k
max_l = 2  # at least length 2

# Try from largest possible even length down to 2
for l in range(total_length, 1, -1):
    if l % 2 == 0:
        n = l // 2
        # Try each possible start position
        found = False
        for i in range(total_length - l + 1):
            valid = True
            for j in range(n):
                pos1 = i + j
                pos2 = i + n + j
                # If both positions are within the original string, they must match
                if pos1 < len(s) and pos2 < len(s):
                    if s[pos1] != s[pos2]:
                        valid = False
                        break
            if valid:
                found = True
                break
        if found:
            max_l = l
            break

Problem

View Problem Statement 
You are an expert Python programmer. You will be given a question (problem specification) and will generate a correct Python program that matches the specification and passes all tests.

Kolya got string s for his birthday, the string consists of small English letters. He immediately added k more characters to the right of the string.

Then Borya came and said that the new string contained a tandem repeat of length l as a substring. How large could l be?

See notes for definition of a tandem repeat.


-----Input-----

The first line contains s (1 ≤ |s| ≤ 200). This string contains only small English letters. The second line contains number k (1 ≤ k ≤ 200) — the number of the added characters.


-----Output-----

Print a single number — the maximum length of the tandem repeat that could have occurred in the new string.


-----Examples-----
Input
aaba
2

Output
6

Input
aaabbbb
2

Output
6

Input
abracadabra
10

Output
20



-----Note-----

A tandem repeat of length 2n is string s, where for any position i (1 ≤ i ≤ n) the following condition fulfills: s_{i} = s_{i} + n.

In the first sample Kolya could obtain a string aabaab, in the second — aaabbbbbb, in the third — abracadabrabracadabra.### Format: Read the inputs from stdin solve the problem and write the answer to stdout (do not directly test on the sample inputs). Enclose your code within delimiters as follows. Ensure that when the python program runs, it reads the inputs, runs the algorithm and writes output to STDOUT.
```python
# YOUR CODE HERE
```

I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have a string `s` and we add `k` more characters to the right
2. After adding these characters, there's a tandem repeat of length `l` as a substring
3. We need to find the maximum possible length `l` of such a tandem repeat

A tandem repeat of length `2n` means a string where the first `n` characters exactly match the second `n` characters. So if we have a tandem repeat of length `l`, then `l` must be even, and we can split it into two equal halves that are identical.

## Key Insights

1. The tandem repeat can start at any position in the new string (original string + k added characters)
2. The tandem repeat can have any length `l` (must be even), but we want the maximum possible
3. Since we can choose what the `k` added characters are, we can potentially create tandem repeats by strategically choosing those characters

## Strategy

1. The tandem repeat must be contained in the final string of length `len(s) + k`
2. For a tandem repeat of length `l` to exist, we need `l ≤ len(s) + k`
3. Since we want maximum length, we should check from the largest possible even length down to 2
4. For each possible even length `l`, we need to check if it's possible to have a tandem repeat of that length in the final string

However, since we can choose the `k` added characters freely, for any tandem repeat of length `l` (where `l` is even), we can try to make it work by:
- Choosing the added characters to match what's needed for the tandem repeat structure
- The tandem repeat can start at position `i` in the final string, where `0 ≤ i ≤ (len(s) + k) - l`

For a tandem repeat of length `l` starting at position `i`, the first half (positions `i` to `i + l//2 - 1`) must equal the second half (positions `i + l//2` to `i + l - 1`).

Since we can choose the last `k` characters, we can always make the second half of a tandem repeat match the first half by setting the appropriate characters in the added part.

However, we need to ensure that the first half doesn't extend beyond the original string or require characters that aren't present.

For a tandem repeat of length `l` starting at position `i`:
- For each position `pos` in `i` to `i + l - 1`:
  - If `pos < len(s)`, we have fixed character `s[pos]`
  - If `pos >= len(s)`, we can set the character to whatever we want

The tandem repeat requires that for each `j` from 0 to `l//2 - 1`:
- The character at position `i + j` equals the character at position `i + l//2 + j`

So for each `j`:
- If `i + j < len(s)` and `i + l//2 + j >= len(s)`, then we can set the character at `i + l//2 + j` to match `s[i + j]`
- If `i + j < len(s)` and `i + l//2 + j < len(s)`, then we need `s[i + j] == s[i + l//2 + j]` 
- If `i + j >= len(s)` and `i + l//2 + j < len(s)`, then we need `s[i + l//2 + j] == (character we set at i + j)`, but since we can set it, we just need to make sure the character we set at `i + j` matches the required one
- If `i + j >= len(s)` and `i + l//2 + j >= len(s)`, then we can set both to match

So a tandem repeat of length `l` starting at position `i` is possible if for every `j` in `0` to `l//2 - 1`, either:
- Both positions `i+j` and `i+l//2+j` are in the original string, and `s[i+j] == s[i+l//2+j]`, OR
- At least one of the positions is in the added part (so we can control it)

So for a given `l`, we just need to check if there exists a starting position `i` such that for every `j` in `[0, l//2)`, the conditions above are satisfied.

But we want the maximum possible `l`.

Algorithm:
1. Start with maximum possible even length: `l_max = len(s) + k`
2. Check if there exists any position `i` such that a tandem repeat of length `l` starting at `i` is possible
3. If yes, return `l`
4. Otherwise, decrease `l` by 2 until we find one that works

But checking all possible even lengths from `len(s)+k` down to 2 is O((len(s)+k)^2), which is acceptable since len(s)+k <= 400.

For each even length `l`, for each possible starting position `i` from 0 to `(len(s) + k) - l`:
  - Check if positions `i` to `i + l - 1` can form a tandem repeat
  - For each `j` from 0 to `l//2 - 1`:
    - pos1 = i + j
    - pos2 = i + l//2 + j
    - if pos1 < len(s) and pos2 < len(s), then check if s[pos1] == s[pos2]
    - if both pos1 and pos2 are >= len(s), we can set both to match
    - if one is < len(s) and one is >= len(s), we can set the one in added part to match
    - Only constraint is when both are in original string and characters don't match
  - If for all j, the condition is satisfied, then tandem repeat of length l is possible

Since we want maximum l, we can start from the largest even length and go down.

Now implement it:

```python
def solve():
    s = input().strip()
    k = int(input().strip())
    n = len(s)
    total_len = n + k
    
    # Try from largest even length down to 2
    max_possible = total_len
    # Start from the largest even length and go down
    for l in range(max_possible, 1, -1):
        if l % 2 == 1:
            continue
            
        # Check if there's any starting position i where tandem repeat of length l is possible
        possible = False
        for i in range(total_len - l + 1):
            valid = True
            half = l // 2
            for j in range(half):
                pos1 = i + j
                pos2 = i + half + j
                
                # Check if both positions are within original string
                if pos1 < n and pos2 < n:
                    if s[pos1] != s[pos2]:
                        valid = False
                        break
                # If both are beyond original string, we can set them to match
                elif pos1 >= n and pos2 >= n:
                    # Both are in added parts, can set to match
                    continue
                # If only one is in original string, we can set the one in added part to match
                else:
                    # One is in original, one is in added part
                    # The one in original has fixed value, the one in added can be set
                    # So we need to ensure the values can be made equal
                    # The value at pos1 if it's in original, or at pos2 if it's in original
                    if pos1 < n and pos2 >= n:
                        # s[pos1] is fixed, we can set s[pos2] to match
                        # So we can always make them equal
                        continue
                    elif pos1 >= n and pos2 < n:
                        # s[pos2] is fixed, we can set s[pos1] to match
                        # So we can always make them equal
                        continue
            if valid:
                possible = True
                break
                
        if possible:
            print(l)
            return
    
    # Should not reach here since l=2 is always possible
    print(2)

solve()
```

So the above logic seems correct.

            # If one is in original and one in added, or both in added, we can make them match
            # Only conflict when both are in original and don't match
        if valid:
            possible = True
            break
            
    if possible:
        print(l)
        break

Submitted Code

View Extracted Code 
s = input().strip()
k = int(input().strip())
n = len(s)
total_len = n + k

# Try from largest even length down to 2
max_possible = total_len
for l in range(max_possible, 1, -1):
    if l % 2 == 1:
        continue
        
    # Check if there's any starting position i where tandem repeat of length l is possible
    possible = False
    for i in range(total_len - l + 1):
        valid = True
        half = l // 2
        for j in range(half):
            pos1 = i + j
            pos2 = i + half + j
            
            # Check if both positions are within original string
            if pos1 < n and pos2 < n:
                if s[pos1] != s[pos2]:
                    valid = False
                    break
            # If one is in original and one in added, or both in added, we can make them match
            # Only conflict when both are in original and don't match
        if valid:
            possible = True
            break
            
    if possible:
        print(l)
        break
